UNDER ARTILLERY FIRE.

Thursday. To-day We had a rather stiff task. The •bri- gade -marched tb Henhan. It was supposed that an enemy's artillery had occupied the hills north of this place, and we were advancing under a heavy gun lire. The 4th South Lanca- shires ocencied the centre of the line, witli.the id ii Liverpools on the left, and the Scottish on the ri,;111. On arrival at effective rifle range, the whole brigade poured an incessant fire on the position, finishing up with a final assault. The movement was carried 4 out, effectively. Later, Colonel Alacfie, in a few well-chosen %\-; )I•Us, pr‘,,S('llted Colonel Fairclough with the ;:up on Tuesday by "A" Company. .He vongr;,:ulated the battalion on winning the cup [Or!he second year in succession. _ _

Two local lady collectors attended/amp to- night on behalf of 'the Denbighshire -Infirmary, and all ranks showed their appreciation of the •many kindnesses received at the hands of the natives by quickly filling their boxes. The semiffmals for the football competition were decided to-night. The Band ,qualified for the final by defeating -" E " Company by three .goals three corners to one goal one corner; and "A" Company- got the better of "C" Company by two goals three corners to one goal one corner.- •

Friday.

The battalion paraded early this morning. Each, man carried 20 roundS of blank ammuni- tion and haversack rations. A. Jong march and a good day's field work was accomplished. The battaliOn returns -home on Sunday, the first train arriving at Warrington shortly after noon. -

MORE MONEY WANTED.

Tne Guardians. to Ask the Overseers for an Increased Levy. _

NO INCREASE IN RATES.

The Warrington Guardians, at their meeting on Wednesday, decided to ask for an increased levy from the Overseers for the ensuing half-year.

They want a sum of Vai11,720, compared with £ll,OOO in the past half-year. This is not expected to lead to any increase in the poor rate.

One member thought the Guardians might save money in different directions, and particu- larly so over vaccination. He said parents were not made aware that they could legally claim exemption from vaccination.

• There were 'several matters of public interest .aralt with by the Warrington Board of Guar- &inns at the fortnightly meeting, held at the Workhouse on Wednesday. In the absence of Mr. E. L. Bent (the chairman), Mr. A. J. Jolley presided, and other members present were: Miss" Broadbent, .Mrs. Simpson,- Mrs. Hall, the Rev. T. R. Matthews, the Rev. A. M. Mitchell, Messrs. G. Mather, W. Flanagan, G. Allen, J. A. Hughes, W. Furness, and T. Dale. The offi- cials in attendance were Mr. A. Bottomley, lerk; Mr. B.!. Partridge, assistant clerk; Mr. W. J. Brown, muster ;.and Mr. C. E. Lawrence, superintendent, Padgate Cottage Homes. - The minutes of the House Committee stated that the Clerk had advertised for a resident medical officer for the Workhouse, at a, salary of £125 per annum, with residential allowances, but had received no applications. It was agreed to offer a.salary Of £l5O.

===== 0002601_19130830_art0250.txt | included=true | included =====
NOT OVERSTAFFED.

It was reported that one of the _nurses hod developed a gastric complaint and .would have to be on leave Of absence for some weeks. The Bev. A. M. MITCHELL: Our nurses are fre- quently differing from' complaints-. The CHAIRMAN: We cannot help it. The nurses' homes are as near ideal as they can be. Mrs. SIMPSON : The conditions are good, but I wonder if they are overworked? Mr. MITCHELL: Do they get sufficient holidays?

The CHAIRMAN: I should think so. They are leniently dealt with, although we are not over- staffed. •

The. subject then dropped

===== 0002601_19130830_art0251.txt | included=true | included =====
MORE MONEY WANTED,

The Finance Committee recommended that the Overseers be aSked to find .t 14,720 for the ensuing half-year ending in March, 1914, in place of £14,000 for the past half-year. The CHAIRMAN said that call upon the Over- seers was higher than it had been for any half- year within the last three years. The Guar- dians had been in the interval using part of their balance in order to obviate an increase in the rates, and now the balance was dangerously low, and some increase was necessary. The amount suggested was about Jive per cent., and the Guardians hoped the balance in the hands of the Overseers of the different townships would obviate the necessity for any increased call upon the 'ratepayers.

The Rev. A. M. MITCHELL was glad to hear that there would be no increase in the rates. He understood the increase was to augment the balance which it,was desirable to have in hand.

The CHAIRMAN: , Our balance is running .too low. . Two years ago it was £4,500, and by this September it will only be £1,380. Mr. MITCHELL: Is there no expenditure we can reduce? In-maintenance is £21710 less, and my impression is that it Ought to be lower than that, having regard to the numbers in the house. There are Also extra medical fees. Having regard to the fact that you are going to give your Medical Officer £l5O a Year, You arc incurring these ,extra; medical expenses. yhe CLInK explained that the' extra-medical fees had nothing to do with the Workhouse. These referred to distriet dOetors, who had, half a guinea for confinements and operations,( and were over and above their salaries.

Mr. MITCHELL: You have-to call in a specialist in such cases.

The CLERK: Oh, no.

===== 0002601_19130830_art0252.txt | included=true | included =====
VACCINATION EXPENSES.

Mr. MITCHELL: I should like to.draW attenti, to the amount put down for Naecination. .\ Sum of ,t 250 seems large. The CLERK: The actual expenditure for the corresponding half-year was £244 Bs. Bd. I am bound to work on these figures. • Mr. ,MITCHELL said it was not known by the people .that they could claim exemption from vaccination. His experience was that the vac- cination 'officers did not. inform the people that they could claim exemption. The officers should not mislead parents that Vaccination was still compulsory. There Was still a great ignorance ,abroad. about that matter, lie thought the Board should take steps to let the people know they could get exemption. Then there were several deputation expenses, amounting to £62 13s. 11d., which might have been curtailed. That was a large sum to spend in trotting about the country. It was not a ease of sour grapes, as he had not had a share of that sum. (Laughter.) The CHAIRMAN: Mr. Mitchell's name appears under that heading Of .expenses. (Laughtev.) Mr. MITCHELL: I beg your pardon—(A Voice: "There's a happy time coming "HI have had a little, but. I took it this meant the expenses of the deputation td the conference. lam a " third-class " man, and I think the Guardians should be satisfied with third-class fares. Mr. G. MATHER said on the yreination forms there was a clause about exemption, which all could read.

Mr. MITCHELL: They can't read. The Finance Committee's recommendation was adopted.

MEDICAL OFFICER'S SALARY.

Mr. G. MATHER moved "That the salary of Dr. A. lThdgliinson, medical officer of .the Work- house, be increased from £l.OO to £l5O per annum." Ile thought no words from him Were, required to recommend such a resolution to the Gua*rd ions.

Mr. MITCHELL asked if the Board had consid- ered the advisability of getting a more experi- enced officer. They ought to get the best medi- cal aid it IS possible to get. It was his opinion that they ought to give their medical attendants better salaries and not 'give so much outside. It would be a wiser thing to have a ,senior than in the House, instead of young doctors. The resolution was carried unanimously.

Does Natural Beauty Make Folk Democratic? RIDES WITH AN OBIEBT.—II. BY SILVER-STREET."

Reading in One of the daily papers about tad' pleasure a gentleman derives from opening his private grounds at Henley-on-Thames to the public reminds me of an opportunity giVen to' us in Warrington of the same, kind,- to those who have a day's holiday deeasibnallP' It is a 60-mile run there and bark to Ness, little village in the Wirral Peninsula, but 133 c, going forward to Rock Ferry, and coming baCl through Liverpool, the ride may be shortenea. to 50 or so. ~ Seven miles beyond. Chester, on' the Itoylake road, a turn to the left brings to Burton, the birth-place of Bishop Isle of Man fame, and half-way betweeit thaV village and Neston a little signboard: .on the'• left of the road reads: "Alt are welcome.;:shutl the gate and keep to the paths." If you Mt° the signpost, you, Cannot miss the smell of sweet briar hedge,- Which Scents the air quite a quarter 61 a mile.. Inside- the gate- 01; eycles may be left if locked, and a wondertan°, , opened for:those:Who-love plants or -deli& In gardens. The gentleman who thus gives free access to his beautiful grounds is, I believer connected with Bees, Ltd., the' great LiVerpoof seedsmen, and is a thorough democrat. lie called himself a Socialist to me once; would that all of that tribe were. like him. It is a very funny story, but will keep till another day, when I have more room. . But every 0114 worth mentioning grows in these gardens, and all are named. The entrance is through per- golas, garlanded with all the newest tarraer roses and clematis. On the left is a water garden, farther on is the rock garden, wit" Alpine plants and acres of wild- gbrse, &c;' form a natural garden as a border to the while the owner's house stands in the centre 013' the top of the hill. The whole is an ~!xane of altruisM, such as one very rarely meets, cially in business life. But I met the thing in Belfast a Week or so ago, at Nurseries. Growing flowers must have tendency to make folks democratic. RIDES IN THE LAKE DISTRICT.
